Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
"""Joint-sparse reconstruction of two sub-sampled sensors sharing DCT components.

Objective (coefficients of both sensors stacked, Z = [Z1; Z2]):

    min_Z  1/2 || BlockDiag(s1 R1 D^H, s2 R2 D^H) Z - [y1; y2] ||_2^2  +  lam * ||Z||_{2,1}

where ||Z||_{2,1} = sum_k sqrt(Z1[k]^2 + Z2[k]^2) couples the sensors so that a
DCT component is either active in both or in none (shared support).
s_i = ||y_i|| * sqrt(N / M_i) is a data-only estimate of ||x_i|| so both sensors
weigh equally in the group norm (sensor 2 is ~5x louder). X_i = s_i * Z_i.

Solver: ProximalGradient (FISTA) with f = L2(Op, b) (smooth) and g = L21(ndim=2).
Step: tau = 1 / ||Op||^2 (power iteration). lam chosen by the discrepancy
principle (data residual ~ sqrt(M) * sigma, sigma = 0.01 noise level). Then the
L21 support is used for a least-squares debiasing refit (removes the shrinkage bias).
"""
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
import matplotlib

matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ setup
sigma = 0.01  # known noise std of the recordings
M1, M2 = len(iava1), len(iava2)
M = M1 + M2
s1 = np.linalg.norm(y1) * np.sqrt(N / M1)  # data-only scale estimates
s2 = np.linalg.norm(y2) * np.sqrt(N / M2)
A1 = Rop1 @ Dop.H
A2 = Rop2 @ Dop.H
Op = pylops.BlockDiag([float(s1) * A1, float(s2) * A2])
y = np.concatenate([y1, y2])
pylops.utils.dottest(Op, M, 2 * N, raiseerror=True)

L = float(np.real(pylops.optimization.eigs.power_iteration(Op.H @ Op, niter=200)[0])) * 1.05
tau = 1.0 / L
print(f"s1={s1:.3f} s2={s2:.3f}  ||Op||^2~{L:.3f}  tau={tau:.4f}")

proxf = pyproximal.L2(Op=Op, b=y)
proxg = pyproximal.L21(ndim=2)
lam_max = float(np.max(np.linalg.norm((Op.H @ y).reshape(2, N), axis=0)))  # Z=0 above this


def solve(lam, niter=2000, callback=None):
    return pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
        proxf, proxg, x0=np.zeros(2 * N), epsg=float(lam), tau=tau,
        niter=niter, acceleration="fista", callback=callback)


# --------------------------------------------- lam via discrepancy principle
# The L21 estimate is shrunk, so its own residual reaches the noise level only
# once many spurious components enter. We therefore apply the discrepancy
# principle to the *debiased* estimate: for each lam take the L21 support,
# refit each sensor by least squares on it, and keep the largest lam (sparsest
# model) whose refit residual is within the noise level sqrt(M)*sigma.
A1d, A2d = A1.todense(), A2.todense()


def support(Zs, n):
    g = np.linalg.norm(Zs.reshape(n, -1), axis=0)
    return np.where(g > 1e-3 * g.max())[0] if g.max() > 0 else np.array([], int)


def refit(Ad, yi, supp):
    Xi = np.zeros(N)
    if len(supp):
        Xi[supp] = np.linalg.lstsq(Ad[:, supp], yi, rcond=None)[0]
    return Xi


target = np.sqrt(M) * sigma
print(f"\nDiscrepancy target (debiased) ||r|| <= sqrt(M)*sigma = {target:.4f}")
print(f"{'lam':>10} {'L21 resid':>10} {'debiased':>9} {'#supp':>6}")
lams = lam_max * np.logspace(-4, -0.05, 40)
chosen = None
for lam_i in lams:
    Zi = solve(lam_i, niter=1000)
    sp = support(Zi, 2)
    rdb = np.sqrt(np.linalg.norm(A1d @ refit(A1d, y1, sp) - y1) ** 2
                  + np.linalg.norm(A2d @ refit(A2d, y2, sp) - y2) ** 2)
    print(f"{lam_i:10.5f} {np.linalg.norm(Op @ Zi - y):10.4f} {rdb:9.4f} {len(sp):6d}")
    if rdb <= target:
        chosen = lam_i
lam = float(chosen if chosen is not None else lams[0])
print(f"chosen lam = {lam:.5f}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------- final solve
cost = []
Z = solve(lam, niter=3000,
          callback=lambda z: cost.append(0.5 * np.linalg.norm(Op @ z - y) ** 2
                                          + lam * proxg(z)))
cost = np.array(cost)
relchg = abs(cost[-1] - cost[-101]) / cost[-1]
print(f"\nFISTA: {len(cost)} its, cost {cost[0]:.4e} -> {cost[-1]:.4e}, "
      f"rel. change over last 100 its {relchg:.1e}")
X1l21, X2l21 = s1 * Z[:N], s2 * Z[N:]

# support from the joint solution, then least-squares debias on that support
supp = support(Z, 2)
print(f"estimated shared support ({len(supp)} comps): {supp.tolist()}")
X1inv = refit(A1d, y1, supp)
X2inv = refit(A2d, y2, supp)
x1inv = Dop.H @ X1inv
x2inv = Dop.H @ X2inv
print(f"debiased residuals: sensor1 {np.linalg.norm(A1 @ X1inv - y1):.4f} "
      f"(expect ~{np.sqrt(M1)*sigma:.4f}), sensor2 {np.linalg.norm(A2 @ X2inv - y2):.4f} "
      f"(expect ~{np.sqrt(M2)*sigma:.4f})")

# ---------------------------------------- baseline: independent L1 per sensor
def l1_single(A, Ad, yi, si, Mi):
    """Independent L1 per sensor, same debiased-discrepancy rule for lam."""
    Ai = float(si) * A
    Li = float(np.real(pylops.optimization.eigs.power_iteration(Ai.H @ Ai, niter=200)[0])) * 1.05
    pf, pg = pyproximal.L2(Op=Ai, b=yi), pyproximal.L1()
    lmax = float(np.max(np.abs(Ai.H @ yi)))
    best = np.zeros(N)
    for li in lmax * np.logspace(-4, -0.05, 40):
        zi = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
            pf, pg, np.zeros(N), epsg=float(li), tau=1 / Li, niter=1000, acceleration="fista")
        Xi = refit(Ad, yi, support(zi, 1))
        if np.linalg.norm(Ad @ Xi - yi) <= np.sqrt(Mi) * sigma:
            best = Xi
    return best


x1_l1 = Dop.H @ l1_single(A1, A1d, y1, s1, M1)
x2_l1 = Dop.H @ l1_single(A2, A2d, y2, s2, M2)

print("\nSNR [dB]                 sensor1   sensor2")
print(f"independent L1 + debias {snr(x1, x1_l1):8.2f}  {snr(x2, x2_l1):8.2f}")
print(f"joint L21               {snr(x1, Dop.H @ X1l21):8.2f}  {snr(x2, Dop.H @ X2l21):8.2f}")
print(f"joint L21 + debias      {snr(x1, x1inv):8.2f}  {snr(x2, x2inv):8.2f}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ plots
fig, axs = plt.subplots(3, 1, figsize=(10, 9))
axs[0].semilogy(cost - cost.min() + 1e-12)
axs[0].set_title("FISTA cost - min (L21 joint)")
for ax, xt, xi, xb, iava, yi, name in [(axs[1], x1, x1inv, x1_l1, iava1, y1, "sensor 1"),
                                       (axs[2], x2, x2inv, x2_l1, iava2, y2, "sensor 2")]:
    ax.plot(xt, "k", lw=3, label="true")
    ax.plot(xb, "--b", label="independent L1 + debias")
    ax.plot(xi, "r", label="joint L21 + debias")
    ax.plot(iava, yi, ".g", label="samples")
    ax.set_title(name)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=8)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig("invert.png", dpi=100)

print(f"SNR sensor 1: {snr(x1, x1inv):.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR sensor 2: {snr(x2, x2inv):.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr(x1, x1inv)}")

      f"(expect ~{np.sqrt(M2)*sigma:.4f})")

# ---------------------------------------- baseline: independent L1 per sensor
def l1_single(A, Ad, yi, si, Mi):
    """Independent L1 per sensor, same debiased-discrepancy rule for lam."""
    Ai = float(si) * A
    Li = float(np.real(pylops.optimization.eigs.power_iteration(Ai.H @ Ai, niter=200)[0])) * 1.05
    pf, pg = pyproximal.L2(Op=Ai, b=yi), pyproximal.L1()

x1_l1 = Dop.H @ l1_single(A1, A1d, y1, s1, M1)
x2_l1 = Dop.H @ l1_single(A2, A2d, y2, s2, M2)

print(f"independent L1 + debias {snr(x1, x1_l1):8.2f}  {snr(x2, x2_l1):8.2f}")
print(f"joint L21               {snr(x1, Dop.H @ X1l21):8.2f}  {snr(x2, Dop.H @ X2l21):8.2f}")
print(f"joint L21 + debias      {snr(x1, x1inv):8.2f}  {snr(x2, x2inv):8.2f}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ plots
fig, axs = plt.subplots(3, 1, figsize=(10, 9))
axs[0].semilogy(cost - cost.min() + 1e-12)
axs[0].set_title("FISTA cost - min (L21 joint)")
for ax, xt, xi, xb, iava, yi, name in [(axs[1], x1, x1inv, x1_l1, iava1, y1, "sensor 1"),
                                       (axs[2], x2, x2inv, x2_l1, iava2, y2, "sensor 2")]:
    ax.plot(xt, "k", lw=3, label="true")
    ax.plot(xb, "--b", label="independent L1 + debias")
    ax.plot(xi, "r", label="joint L21 + debias")
    ax.plot(iava, yi, ".g", label="samples")
    ax.set_title(name)
    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=8)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig("invert.png", dpi=100)

print(f"SNR sensor 1: {snr(x1, x1inv):.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR sensor 2: {snr(x2, x2inv):.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr(x1, x1inv)}")

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I wrote and ran `invert.py`. **For sensor 1 it gets an SNR of 24.19 dB, and 47.26 dB for sensor 2.** The last line it prints is `SNR_DB=24.18699873290479`.

**What I chose**

- **Unknowns:** the DCT coefficients of both sensors, solved together as one problem, X = [X1; X2].
- **Objective:** `min_X ½‖BlockDiag(R1·Dᴴ, R2·Dᴴ) X − [y1; y2]‖² + σ Σ_k ‖(X1[k], X2[k])‖₂`
  - The first term is the fit to the samples you have.
  - The second term (an L21, or "group sparsity", penalty) pushes each frequency to be either on in both sensors or off in both. This lets sensor 2, with 60% of its samples, show where the components are, which sensor 1 (8%) can't work out on its own.
- **Solver:** PyProximal's `ProximalGradient` with FISTA acceleration, using `L2(Op, b)` for the data term and `σ·L21(ndim=2)` for the penalty. The operator passes the dot-test.
- **Step size:** the operator norm squared, ‖Op‖², measured by power iteration, is 1.0. I inflated it by 5%, giving `tau = 0.952`.
- **Debiasing:** the L21 penalty shrinks all amplitudes, and sensor 1's small ones suffer most. So after finding the shared support, I re-fit each sensor by ordinary least squares on just those frequencies.
- **Choosing σ:** I swept σ downward and took the first value where the debiased residual drops to the noise level (residual energy ≤ M·0.01²). That gave σ = 0.97. It uses only the known noise level (0.01), never the true signals. The SNR columns in the sweep are printed as a diagnostic only.

**How well it worked**

- **Support:** it found exactly the 6 true frequencies [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]. Nothing in the method was told the number of components.
- **Convergence:** FISTA stopped after 18 iterations. The cost went from 10.94 to 10.57, and the relative change in the last iteration was 9e-11.
- **Comparison with simpler methods** (sensor 1 / sensor 2):

| Method | Sensor 1 | Sensor 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Separate sparsity (L1) problem for each sensor | 0.36 dB | 38.8 dB |
| Joint L21, no debiasing | 0.71 dB | 4.5 dB |
| **Joint L21 + debiasing (final)** | **24.19 dB** | **47.26 dB** |

On its own, sensor 1's 20 samples are not enough to find the components. Sharing the support with sensor 2 is what makes its reconstruction possible. The debiasing step is also essential, because without it the shrinkage wipes out most of the accuracy.

**Things you could still tune**

- The σ grid is coarse (25 log-spaced values), and the support threshold is 1e-3 of the largest group norm.
- With real data you would need to estimate the noise level rather than use the known 0.01.
- Sensor 1's remaining error mainly comes from fitting 6 amplitudes to 20 noisy samples, so a different penalty is unlikely to improve it much.

The script also saves `invert.png`, which shows the cost curve and both reconstructions against the true signals and samples.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

# ----------------------------------------------------------------- setup (given)
np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ formulation
# Unknowns: DCT coefficients X = [X1; X2] (2N). Both sensors share the support,
# so we use joint (group) sparsity:
#   min_X  1/2 || BlockDiag(R1 D^H, R2 D^H) X - [y1; y2] ||^2 + sigma * sum_k ||(X1[k], X2[k])||_2
# solved with FISTA (ProximalGradient), f = L2 (smooth), g = sigma * L21(ndim=2).
# The L21 prox couples the two sensors frequency by frequency: sensor 2 (60 %
# of samples) tells where the components are, sensor 1 (8 %) benefits from it.
# A final least-squares debiasing on the detected joint support removes the
# shrinkage bias of the L21 prox (which otherwise hits the small sensor-1
# amplitudes hardest).
sigman = 0.01  # noise std, known from the acquisition (used for discrepancy)
Op1 = Rop1 * Dop.H
Op2 = Rop2 * Dop.H
Op = pylops.BlockDiag([Op1, Op2])
y = np.concatenate([y1, y2])
M = len(y)
assert pylops.utils.dottest(Op, M, 2 * N)

# Lipschitz constant of grad f: ||Op||^2 (=1 for restriction of orthonormal DCT)
L = pylops.optimization.eigs.power_iteration(Op.H @ Op, niter=100)[0].real * 1.05
tau = 1.0 / L
print(f"||Op||^2 ~ {L / 1.05:.4f}  ->  tau = {tau:.4f}")

proxf = pyproximal.L2(Op=Op, b=y)


def solve_joint(sig, niter=500, tol=1e-10):
    cost = []
    proxg = float(sig) * pyproximal.L21(ndim=2)
    cb = lambda x: cost.append(proxf(x) + proxg(x))
    X = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
        proxf, proxg, x0=np.zeros(2 * N), tau=tau, niter=niter,
        acceleration="fista", tol=tol, callback=cb)
    return X, np.array(cost)


def debias(X, thresh_rel=1e-3):
    """LS re-fit of each sensor on the jointly detected support."""
    Xs = X.reshape(2, N)
    gnorm = np.linalg.norm(Xs, axis=0)
    supp = np.where(gnorm > thresh_rel * gnorm.max())[0]
    Xd = np.zeros_like(Xs)
    if gnorm.max() == 0:
        return Xd.ravel(), supp[:0]
    supp = np.where(gnorm > thresh_rel * gnorm.max())[0]
    for k, (Opk, yk) in enumerate([(Op1, y1), (Op2, y2)]):
        A = (Opk @ np.eye(N)[:, supp])  # columns of the operator on support
        Xd[k, supp] = np.linalg.lstsq(A, yk, rcond=None)[0]
    return Xd.ravel(), supp


# ---------------------------------------- choose sigma: discrepancy principle
# largest sigma whose *debiased* residual reaches the noise level
# ||Op X - y||^2 <= M sigman^2 (no use of the true model).
sigmas = np.logspace(1, -3, 25) * np.abs(Op.H @ y).max()
target = M * sigman**2
print("\n   sigma      |supp|  ||r||^2/(M sn^2)   [diag: SNR1  SNR2]")
chosen = None
for s in sigmas:
    Xs, _ = solve_joint(s, niter=300)
    Xd, supp = debias(Xs)
    ratio = np.sum((Op @ Xd - y) ** 2) / target
    d1, d2 = snr(x1, Dop.H @ Xd[:N]), snr(x2, Dop.H @ Xd[N:])
    print(f"  {s:9.4f}  {len(supp):5d}   {ratio:10.3f}        {d1:6.2f} {d2:6.2f}")
    if ratio <= 1.0 and len(supp) > 0:
        chosen = s
        break
if chosen is None:
    chosen = sigmas[-1]
print(f"chosen sigma = {chosen:.4f} (discrepancy principle)")

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ final solve
Xjoint, cost = solve_joint(chosen, niter=1000)
print(f"\nFISTA iterations: {len(cost)}, cost start {cost[0]:.4e} -> end {cost[-1]:.4e}, "
      f"rel. change last iter {abs(cost[-1] - cost[-2]) / cost[-2]:.2e}")
Xfinal, supp = debias(Xjoint)
print("detected joint support:", supp.tolist())
x1inv = Dop.H @ Xfinal[:N]
x2inv = Dop.H @ Xfinal[N:]

# --------------------------------------------- baselines (diagnostic only)
# (a) joint L21 without debiasing; (b) independent L1 per sensor (FISTA),
# each with its own sigma from the discrepancy principle.
def solve_l1(Opk, yk, niter=1000):
    pf = pyproximal.L2(Op=Opk, b=yk)
    for s in np.logspace(1, -3, 25) * np.abs(Opk.H @ yk).max():
        Xk = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
            pf, float(s) * pyproximal.L1(), x0=np.zeros(N), tau=tau,
            niter=niter, acceleration="fista", tol=1e-10)
        if np.sum((Opk @ Xk - yk) ** 2) <= len(yk) * sigman**2:
            break
    return Xk

X1l1 = solve_l1(Op1, y1)
X2l1 = solve_l1(Op2, y2)
print("\nBaselines:")
print(f"  independent L1   : SNR1 = {snr(x1, Dop.H @ X1l1):6.2f} dB, SNR2 = {snr(x2, Dop.H @ X2l1):6.2f} dB")
print(f"  joint L21 (biased): SNR1 = {snr(x1, Dop.H @ Xjoint[:N]):6.2f} dB, "
      f"SNR2 = {snr(x2, Dop.H @ Xjoint[N:]):6.2f} dB")

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(3, 1, figsize=(9, 9))
    axs[0].semilogy(cost, "k")
    axs[0].set_title("Joint L21 FISTA cost")
    for ax, x, xi, iava, yk, name in [(axs[1], x1, x1inv, iava1, y1, "Sensor 1"),
                                      (axs[2], x2, x2inv, iava2, y2, "Sensor 2")]:
        ax.plot(x, "k", lw=3, label="true")
        ax.plot(xi, "r", lw=1.5, label="joint L21 + debias")
        ax.plot(iava, yk, ".b", label="samples")
        ax.set_title(name)
        ax.legend(loc="upper right")
    fig.tight_layout()
    fig.savefig("invert.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

snr1 = snr(x1, x1inv)
snr2 = snr(x2, x2inv)
print(f"\nSensor 1 SNR: {snr1:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR: {snr2:.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr1}")

      f"rel. change last iter {abs(cost[-1] - cost[-2]) / cost[-2]:.2e}")

    return Xk

X1l1 = solve_l1(Op1, y1)
X2l1 = solve_l1(Op2, y2)
print("\nBaselines:")
print(f"  independent L1   : SNR1 = {snr(x1, Dop.H @ X1l1):6.2f} dB, SNR2 = {snr(x2, Dop.H @ X2l1):6.2f} dB")
print(f"  joint L21 (biased): SNR1 = {snr(x1, Dop.H @ Xjoint[:N]):6.2f} dB, "
      f"SNR2 = {snr(x2, Dop.H @ Xjoint[N:]):6.2f} dB")

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(3, 1, figsize=(9, 9))
    axs[0].semilogy(cost, "k")
    axs[0].set_title("Joint L21 FISTA cost")
    for ax, x, xi, iava, yk, name in [(axs[1], x1, x1inv, iava1, y1, "Sensor 1"),
                                      (axs[2], x2, x2inv, iava2, y2, "Sensor 2")]:
        ax.plot(x, "k", lw=3, label="true")
        ax.plot(xi, "r", lw=1.5, label="joint L21 + debias")
        ax.plot(iava, yk, ".b", label="samples")
        ax.set_title(name)
        ax.legend(loc="upper right")
    fig.tight_layout()
    fig.savefig("invert.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

snr1 = snr(x1, x1inv)
snr2 = snr(x2, x2inv)
print(f"\nSensor 1 SNR: {snr1:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR: {snr2:.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr1}")

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I wrote `invert.py` and ran it. **Sensor 1 reconstructs at 24.2 dB SNR and sensor 2 at 47.3 dB.** The last line it prints is `SNR_DB=24.18699873290469`.

**What I chose: joint sparse inversion.** The two sensors use the same DCT components, so I solved for both sets of coefficients together.

- **Model:** the unknowns are `[X1; X2]`, linked to the data by `BlockDiag(R1·DCTᴴ, R2·DCTᴴ)`.
- **Penalty:** a group-sparsity penalty (L2,1, `pyproximal.L21(ndim=2)`, ε=0.05) treats each DCT coefficient k as a pair `(X1[k], X2[k])`. Pairs are switched on or off together, but each sensor keeps its own amplitudes. With 60% of its samples, sensor 2 effectively tells sensor 1 which components are present, so its 20 samples only need to fix 6 amplitudes.
- **Solver:** FISTA (`ProximalGradient`, 500 iterations), with step size 0.99. The operator has norm 1 because it is a sample selection applied to an orthonormal DCT.
- **Debiasing:** the penalty shrinks amplitudes. So I took the shared set of components it found (any group above 5% of the largest) and refit each sensor by least squares on that set alone.

**Results:**

| | Sensor 1 | Sensor 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Joint group-sparse only | 12.45 dB | 30.26 dB |
| + least-squares refit | **24.19 dB** | **47.26 dB** |

It found exactly the right components: `[4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]`.

**Why the joint approach matters:** as a separate check (not in the script), I reconstructed sensor 1 alone with a standard sparse (L1) solver. It reached only about 0.5 dB SNR at every penalty strength I tried, which means it failed. With only 20 noisy samples, sensor 1 can't find its own components; borrowing them from sensor 2 is what makes it work.

The refit step relies on the components being detected correctly, which they were here. If the noise were higher or a component very weak, the 5% cutoff might need tuning. Without the refit, the group-sparse result alone gives 12.45 dB for sensor 1.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal

np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Joint-sparse (multiple measurement vector) inversion
#
# Unknown: DCT coefficients of both sensors stacked, X = [X1; X2] (2N).
# Forward: blockdiag(R1 D^H, R2 D^H). Both sensors share the same support,
# so we use an L2,1 (group) penalty, grouping coefficient k of sensor 1 with
# coefficient k of sensor 2:
#     min_X 1/2 ||G X - y||_2^2 + eps * sum_k sqrt(X1[k]^2 + X2[k]^2)
# The well-sampled sensor 2 pins down the support, which lets sensor 1 be
# recovered from only 20 samples.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
Op1 = Rop1 @ Dop.H
Op2 = Rop2 @ Dop.H
Gop = pylops.BlockDiag([Op1, Op2])
y = np.concatenate([y1, y2])

eps = 0.05
l2 = pyproximal.L2(Op=Gop, b=y)
l21 = pyproximal.L21(ndim=2, sigma=eps)  # reshapes X to (2, N), norm over axis 0
tau = 0.99  # ||G||^2 = 1 (restriction of an orthonormal transform)

Xinv = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
    l2, l21, x0=np.zeros(2 * N), tau=tau, niter=500, acceleration="fista"
)
X1inv, X2inv = Xinv[:N], Xinv[N:]

# Debiasing: least-squares refit on the detected common support removes the
# amplitude shrinkage introduced by the L2,1 penalty.
gnorm = np.sqrt(X1inv**2 + X2inv**2)
support = np.where(gnorm > 0.05 * gnorm.max())[0]
print(f"Detected support: {support.tolist()} (true: {icomp})")

A1 = Op1.todense()[:, support]
A2 = Op2.todense()[:, support]
X1d = np.zeros(N)
X2d = np.zeros(N)
X1d[support] = np.linalg.lstsq(A1, y1, rcond=None)[0]
X2d[support] = np.linalg.lstsq(A2, y2, rcond=None)[0]

x1inv = Dop.H @ X1d
x2inv = Dop.H @ X2d

snr1 = pylops.utils.metrics.snr(x1, x1inv)
snr2 = pylops.utils.metrics.snr(x2, x2inv)
snr1_nodebias = pylops.utils.metrics.snr(x1, Dop.H @ X1inv)
snr2_nodebias = pylops.utils.metrics.snr(x2, Dop.H @ X2inv)
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR (L2,1 only): {snr1_nodebias:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR (L2,1 only): {snr2_nodebias:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR: {snr1:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR: {snr2:.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr1}")
✓ pass objective-formulation

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I saved the script as `invert.py` and ran it. Sensor 1 comes back at **24.2 dB SNR**, compared with 0.4 dB when sensor 1 is reconstructed on its own.

**Approach:** both sensors share the same DCT components, so I reconstructed them together (joint sparsity) rather than one at a time.

1. **Joint problem.** I solve for both sets of DCT coefficients at once. The data term covers both sensors' samples. An L2,1 penalty groups each DCT index k as the pair (X1[k], X2[k]), so a component is either used by both sensors or by neither. In practice, sensor 2 (60% of samples) finds which components are present, and sensor 1 (8%, about 20 samples) uses that. Solved with FISTA (`pyproximal.ProximalGradient`, `L2` + `L21(ndim=2)`).
2. **Scaling.** I divide each sensor's data by its own maximum, so sensor 2's larger amplitudes don't dominate the grouping. This only changes how the penalty weighs the two sensors; the original scales are restored afterwards.
3. **Least-squares refit.** I keep the components whose group strength is above 10% of the largest, then refit each sensor's amplitudes by plain least squares on just those components. This undoes the shrinkage the penalty adds. With only 6 unknowns, sensor 1's ~20 samples are plenty for this.

**Results** (from the run):

| | SNR |
|---|---|
| Sensor 1 alone, plain L1 (baseline for comparison) | 0.36 dB |
| **Sensor 1, joint + refit** | **24.19 dB** |
| Sensor 2, joint + refit | 47.26 dB |

The detected components were exactly the true set `[4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]`. Sensor 1 alone fails because 20 noisy samples aren't enough to pin down which components are present. Once sensor 2 supplies that, sensor 1 is a small, well-posed fit. Its remaining error comes mostly from measurement noise on only 20 samples.

The script ends with `SNR_DB=24.18699873290469`.

**Caveats:** the penalty weight (`eps=0.05`) and the 10% cutoff were picked by hand for this synthetic case, not tuned. The method also depends on your assumption that the two sensors really do share the same components. If one sensor had extra components the other lacks, the joint penalty would tend to suppress them.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Joint (group-sparse) reconstruction
#
# Both signals share the same DCT support, so we solve for the two DCT
# coefficient vectors jointly with an L2,1 penalty: for every DCT index k the
# pair (X1[k], X2[k]) is either jointly zero or jointly active.
#
#   min_X  1/2 || [R1 D^H, 0; 0, R2 D^H] [X1; X2] - [y1; y2] ||_2^2
#          + eps * sum_k sqrt(X1[k]^2 + X2[k]^2)
#
# Sensor 2 (60% of samples, large amplitudes) effectively identifies the
# support, which sensor 1 (only 8% samples) can then borrow.
# Each sensor's data is normalised to unit max so neither dominates the
# group norm purely because of its amplitude scale.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
s1 = np.abs(y1).max()
s2 = np.abs(y2).max()

Op = pylops.BlockDiag([Rop1 @ Dop.H, Rop2 @ Dop.H])
y = np.concatenate([y1 / s1, y2 / s2])

# FISTA with pyproximal: f = L2 data misfit, g = L2,1 over (2, N) layout
f = pyproximal.L2(Op=Op, b=y)
eps = 0.05
g = pyproximal.L21(ndim=2, sigma=eps)
tau = 0.99 / np.abs((Op.H @ Op).eigs(neigs=1, symmetric=True)[0])

Xjoint = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
    f, g, x0=np.zeros(2 * N), tau=tau, niter=500, acceleration="fista", show=False
)
X1inv, X2inv = Xjoint[:N] * s1, Xjoint[N:] * s2

# Debiasing: least-squares refit of the amplitudes on the detected common
# support (removes the shrinkage bias of the L2,1 penalty).
gnorm = np.sqrt((X1inv / s1) ** 2 + (X2inv / s2) ** 2)
support = np.where(gnorm > 0.1 * gnorm.max())[0]
print(f"Detected common support: {support.tolist()} (true: {icomp})")

for R, yy, Xinv in [(Rop1, y1, X1inv), (Rop2, y2, X2inv)]:
    A = (R @ Dop.H).todense()[:, support]
    Xinv[:] = 0.0
    Xinv[support] = np.linalg.lstsq(A, yy, rcond=None)[0]

x1inv = Dop.H @ X1inv
x2inv = Dop.H @ X2inv

# Baseline for comparison: sensor 1 alone with plain L1 (ISTA/FISTA)
x1ind = Dop.H @ pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
    Rop1 @ Dop.H, y1, niter=500, eps=1e-2
)[0]

snr1 = snr(x1, x1inv)
snr2 = snr(x2, x2inv)
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR (independent L1 baseline): {snr(x1, x1ind):.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR (joint L2,1 + debias):     {snr1:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR (joint L2,1 + debias):     {snr2:.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr1}")

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I saved the script as `invert.py` and ran it. Sensor 1 comes back at **24.2 dB SNR**, compared with 0.4 dB when sensor 1 is reconstructed on its own.

**Approach:** both sensors share the same DCT components, so I reconstructed them together (joint sparsity) rather than one at a time.

1. **Joint problem.** I solve for both sets of DCT coefficients at once. The data term covers both sensors' samples. An L2,1 penalty groups each DCT index k as the pair (X1[k], X2[k]), so a component is either used by both sensors or by neither. In practice, sensor 2 (60% of samples) finds which components are present, and sensor 1 (8%, about 20 samples) uses that. Solved with FISTA (`pyproximal.ProximalGradient`, `L2` + `L21(ndim=2)`).
2. **Scaling.** I divide each sensor's data by its own maximum, so sensor 2's larger amplitudes don't dominate the grouping. This only changes how the penalty weighs the two sensors; the original scales are restored afterwards.
3. **Least-squares refit.** I keep the components whose group strength is above 10% of the largest, then refit each sensor's amplitudes by plain least squares on just those components. This undoes the shrinkage the penalty adds. With only 6 unknowns, sensor 1's ~20 samples are plenty for this.

**Results** (from the run):

| | SNR |
|---|---|
| Sensor 1 alone, plain L1 (baseline for comparison) | 0.36 dB |
| **Sensor 1, joint + refit** | **24.19 dB** |
| Sensor 2, joint + refit | 47.26 dB |

The detected components were exactly the true set `[4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]`. Sensor 1 alone fails because 20 noisy samples aren't enough to pin down which components are present. Once sensor 2 supplies that, sensor 1 is a small, well-posed fit. Its remaining error comes mostly from measurement noise on only 20 samples.

The script ends with `SNR_DB=24.18699873290469`.

**Caveats:** the penalty weight (`eps=0.05`) and the 10% cutoff were picked by hand for this synthetic case, not tuned. The method also depends on your assumption that the two sensors really do share the same components. If one sensor had extra components the other lacks, the joint penalty would tend to suppress them.
✓ pass script-written-and-run ×0.5

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Joint (group-sparse) reconstruction
#
# Both signals share the same DCT support, so we solve for the two DCT
# coefficient vectors jointly with an L2,1 penalty: for every DCT index k the
# pair (X1[k], X2[k]) is either jointly zero or jointly active.
#
#   min_X  1/2 || [R1 D^H, 0; 0, R2 D^H] [X1; X2] - [y1; y2] ||_2^2
#          + eps * sum_k sqrt(X1[k]^2 + X2[k]^2)
#
# Sensor 2 (60% of samples, large amplitudes) effectively identifies the
# support, which sensor 1 (only 8% samples) can then borrow.
# Each sensor's data is normalised to unit max so neither dominates the
# group norm purely because of its amplitude scale.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
s1 = np.abs(y1).max()
s2 = np.abs(y2).max()

Op = pylops.BlockDiag([Rop1 @ Dop.H, Rop2 @ Dop.H])
y = np.concatenate([y1 / s1, y2 / s2])

# FISTA with pyproximal: f = L2 data misfit, g = L2,1 over (2, N) layout
f = pyproximal.L2(Op=Op, b=y)
eps = 0.05
g = pyproximal.L21(ndim=2, sigma=eps)
tau = 0.99 / np.abs((Op.H @ Op).eigs(neigs=1, symmetric=True)[0])

Xjoint = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
    f, g, x0=np.zeros(2 * N), tau=tau, niter=500, acceleration="fista", show=False
)
X1inv, X2inv = Xjoint[:N] * s1, Xjoint[N:] * s2

# Debiasing: least-squares refit of the amplitudes on the detected common
# support (removes the shrinkage bias of the L2,1 penalty).
gnorm = np.sqrt((X1inv / s1) ** 2 + (X2inv / s2) ** 2)
support = np.where(gnorm > 0.1 * gnorm.max())[0]
print(f"Detected common support: {support.tolist()} (true: {icomp})")

for R, yy, Xinv in [(Rop1, y1, X1inv), (Rop2, y2, X2inv)]:
    A = (R @ Dop.H).todense()[:, support]
    Xinv[:] = 0.0
    Xinv[support] = np.linalg.lstsq(A, yy, rcond=None)[0]

x1inv = Dop.H @ X1inv
x2inv = Dop.H @ X2inv

# Baseline for comparison: sensor 1 alone with plain L1 (ISTA/FISTA)
x1ind = Dop.H @ pylops.optimization.sparsity.fista(
    Rop1 @ Dop.H, y1, niter=500, eps=1e-2
)[0]

snr1 = snr(x1, x1inv)
snr2 = snr(x2, x2inv)
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR (independent L1 baseline): {snr(x1, x1ind):.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR (joint L2,1 + debias):     {snr1:.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR (joint L2,1 + debias):     {snr2:.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr1}")

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I wrote `invert.py` and ran it. Sensor 1 comes out at about **24.2 dB SNR** and sensor 2 at about **47.3 dB**.

**Approach**

1. **Solve for both sensors together with a shared-support penalty.** Since both signals use the same DCT components, I solve for both sets of coefficients at once. The penalty at each DCT index is the length of the pair (sensor 1 value, sensor 2 value), so a component is either switched on for both sensors or off for both. Sensor 2's 154 samples work out which components are active, and sensor 1's 20 samples then only have to set the amplitudes. I solved it with PyProximal's FISTA solver (`ProximalGradient`), using `L2(Op=BlockDiag([R1·Dᴴ, R2·Dᴴ]), b=[y1; y2])` for the data fit and `L21(ndim=2, sigma=0.1)` for the penalty. The step size is 0.95, which is safe because the operator's norm is at most 1. It runs 500 iterations.
2. **Correct the amplitudes.** This kind of penalty shrinks amplitudes. Sensor 1 is hit hardest because its values are small next to sensor 2's in each pair. So I take the components the first step found and refit each sensor by plain least squares on just those components.

**Results**

| Method | Sensor 1 SNR | Sensor 2 SNR |
|---|---|---|
| Sensor 1 alone (L1 sparsity, for comparison) | 0.40 dB | – |
| Joint, before amplitude correction | 8.37 dB | 24.28 dB |
| **Joint + amplitude correction (final)** | **24.19 dB** | **47.26 dB** |

The joint step found exactly the true components `[4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]`. Reconstructing sensor 1 on its own fails: 20 noisy samples aren't enough to find the components without sensor 2's help. The amplitude correction is what raised sensor 1 from about 8 dB to 24 dB. The last line printed is `SNR_DB=24.18699873290469`.

**Limitations**

- I only tested this one setup. The penalty weight (0.1) and the 1% cutoff used to decide which components count as active weren't tuned or checked on other seeds.
- The amplitude correction trusts that the found components are right. If the joint step missed a component or added an extra one, the refit would carry that error into both signals. Here the components were exactly right.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal

np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Joint-sparsity (group / L21) inversion
#
# Both signals share the same DCT support, so we invert for the stacked
# coefficients X = [X1; X2] and penalise the L2 norm across sensors of each
# DCT coefficient (L21). Sensor 2 (60% samples) effectively tells sensor 1
# (8% samples, i.e. only 20 samples) where the active components are.
#
#   min_X 0.5 ||G X - y||_2^2 + eps * sum_i sqrt(X1[i]^2 + X2[i]^2)
#   G = BlockDiag(R1 D^H, R2 D^H)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
Op1 = Rop1 * Dop.H
Op2 = Rop2 * Dop.H
Gop = pylops.BlockDiag([Op1, Op2])
y = np.concatenate([y1, y2])

# Lipschitz constant of the gradient: ||G||^2 = 1 (restriction of an
# orthonormal transform), so step tau = 1 is safe; use 0.95 for margin.
tau = 0.95
eps = 0.1
l2 = pyproximal.L2(Op=Gop, b=y)
l21 = pyproximal.L21(ndim=2, sigma=eps)  # groups: (X1[i], X2[i]) pairs

Xinv = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
    l2, l21, x0=np.zeros(2 * N), epsg=1.0, tau=tau, niter=500,
    acceleration="fista", show=False,
)
X1l21, X2l21 = Xinv[:N], Xinv[N:]

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Debiasing: L21 shrinks amplitudes (bias). Use the recovered joint support
# and refit each sensor with plain least squares restricted to that support.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
gnorm = np.sqrt(X1l21**2 + X2l21**2)
support = np.where(gnorm > 1e-2 * gnorm.max())[0]
print(f"Recovered support: {support.tolist()}  (true: {icomp})")


def debias(Op, yobs, supp):
    A = Op.todense()[:, supp]
    coeffs = np.linalg.lstsq(A, yobs, rcond=None)[0]
    X = np.zeros(N)
    X[supp] = coeffs
    return X


X1inv = debias(Op1, y1, support)
X2inv = debias(Op2, y2, support)
x1inv = Dop.H @ X1inv
x2inv = Dop.H @ X2inv

# Baseline for comparison: independent L1 (ISTA/FISTA) on sensor 1 only
X1ind = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
    pyproximal.L2(Op=Op1, b=y1), pyproximal.L1(sigma=0.01),
    x0=np.zeros(N), tau=tau, niter=500, acceleration="fista", show=False,
)
x1ind = Dop.H @ X1ind

snr = pylops.utils.metrics.snr
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR, independent L1 (baseline): {snr(x1, x1ind):.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR, joint L21 (no debias):     {snr(x1, Dop.H @ X1l21):.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR, joint L21 (no debias):     {snr(x2, Dop.H @ X2l21):.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 1 SNR, joint L21 + debias:        {snr(x1, x1inv):.2f} dB")
print(f"Sensor 2 SNR, joint L21 + debias:        {snr(x2, x2inv):.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR_DB={snr(x1, x1inv)}")

Prompt

Two sensors record the same phenomenon: their signals are made of the same few cosine (DCT) components, just with different amplitudes. Sensor 1 lost most of its samples (only 8% kept), sensor 2 kept 60%. Both recordings are slightly noisy. I want to reconstruct both full signals from the samples I have. Here is my synthetic setup:

import numpy as np
import pylops

np.random.seed(10)
N = 256
icomp = [4, 8, 11, 17, 23, 30]
amps1 = [1.0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.7, 0.3, 0.6]
amps2 = [3.0, 3.0, 2.0, 2.5, 2.0, 3.0]
Dop = pylops.signalprocessing.DCT(N)  # orthonormal DCT

X1 = np.zeros(N)
X2 = np.zeros(N)
X1[icomp] = amps1
X2[icomp] = amps2
x1 = Dop.H @ X1  # true signal of sensor 1
x2 = Dop.H @ X2  # true signal of sensor 2

iava1 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.08))])
iava2 = np.sort(np.random.permutation(N)[: int(round(N * 0.6))])
Rop1 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava1)
Rop2 = pylops.Restriction(N, iava2)
y1 = Rop1 @ x1 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava1))  # observed samples of sensor 1
y2 = Rop2 @ x2 + 0.01 * np.random.randn(len(iava2))  # observed samples of sensor 2

Sensor 1 is the one I care about most. Please write a runnable Python script (starting from the setup above) that reconstructs both signals x1inv and x2inv, save it as invert.py in the current folder, run it, and tell me what you chose and how well it worked. The script must print the SNR of each sensor, and as its last line SNR_DB=<value> where the value is pylops.utils.metrics.snr(x1, x1inv) for sensor 1.

Using the observed samples, the sampling indices iava1/iava2, the operators, the noise level 0.01 and the general statement that the two signals share the same few DCT components is allowed.

Showing independent L1 reconstructions only as a comparison is fine.

In addition, the final model must be computed with a proximal algorithm from PyProximal — a solver in pyproximal.optimization (ProximalGradient, ADMM, ADMML2, LinearizedADMM, PrimalDual, …) with PyProximal proximal operators. You are shown the script invert.py.

Claim: every step size of the iterative solver used for the final model (tau, mu, alpha, a learning rate, …) is derived from a quantity computed or justified for this operator — the Lipschitz constant / squared operator norm ‖Op‖² (or ‖K‖² for a stacked operator) obtained with power iteration, eigs, an SVD/np.linalg.norm of the explicit matrix, or an analytic bound written in a comment (e.g. "a restriction of an orthonormal DCT has ‖Op‖ ≤ 1") — or the solver uses backtracking / line search (e.g. tau=None in pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient). A solver that needs no step size (e.g. scipy lsq_linear, a direct solve) also makes the claim TRUE.

The claim is FALSE if a step size is a hard-coded number (e.g. tau=0.1, tau=1.0) with no computation or written justification tied to the operator norm.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I wrote `invert.py`, ran it (about 11 s), and it fills in the missing entries with an SNR of **33.83 dB**. The last line it prints is `SNR_DB=33.8295946670265`.

**Objective**

  min_X ½‖R vec(X) − y‖² + g(singular values of X)

`R` is `pylops.Restriction`, which picks out the 1,080 observed entries.

**What I chose and why**

- **Solver:** PyProximal's `ProximalGradient` with FISTA acceleration. The data term is smooth, and the penalty on the singular values has a cheap update based on one SVD per iteration.
- **Step size:** τ = 0.99/‖R‖². A power iteration gives ‖R‖² = 1, so τ = 0.99.
- **Stage 1, convex:** g = λ₁‖X‖_*, the nuclear norm (sum of singular values), which is the standard choice for low-rank completion. On its own it reaches only **20.65 dB**, and it leaves rank 17 where the table should have rank 3. A sweep of λ₁ scored against the true matrix never got above about 20.6 dB, so the cause is that the nuclear norm shrinks every singular value, including the large ones. The choice of λ₁ is not the problem.
- **Stage 2, non-convex:** g = SCAD penalty on each singular value (`pyproximal.SingularValuePenalty` with `pyproximal.SCAD`), started from the stage 1 result. SCAD removes small singular values but leaves large ones unshrunk. The result has rank 3, with an RMS residual of 0.0406 on the observed entries.
- **Choosing λ₁ and λ₂:** 5-fold cross-validation on the observed entries only: fit on four fifths of them and score the prediction of the held-out fifth. The truth and the true rank are never used, and the rank comes out as 3 on its own. The candidate values are fractions of the largest singular value of the zero-filled matrix. The chosen values were λ₁ = 0.19 and λ₂ = 1.71. The held-out error at λ₂ is 0.0695, against about 0.355 for the nuclear norm alone.
- **Consistency check:** the residual of 0.0406 is about what you'd expect from noise of 0.05 once you allow for the parameters a rank-3 fit uses: 0.05·√(1 − 351/1080) ≈ 0.041.

**Results**

| | SNR |
|---|---|
| Zero-filled baseline | 1.53 dB |
| Nuclear norm only | 20.65 dB |
| Nuclear norm then SCAD (final) | **33.83 dB** |

**Convergence**

- Stage 1 stopped on the tolerance after 530 iterations; the objective went from 59.19 to 34.02.
- Stage 2 used all 1,500 iterations without meeting the 1e-10 tolerance, but the objective was flat by then: 24.65 to 21.52, with a last relative change of 5e-9.
- Plots of the cost curves, the cross-validation curves and the final singular values are saved to `invert_diagnostics.png`.

**Things to know**

- SCAD is non-convex, so the result depends on the starting point. Starting from the stage 1 solution matters.
- The SCAD cross-validation curve is bumpy at mid-range λ, where it is still keeping a few extra singular values. It levels off once λ₂ is large enough to remove them; the two largest candidates (1.71 and 2.80) give the same result.
- Values you could still tune are the shape parameter of SCAD (left at the default a = 3.7), the number of folds and the λ grids.

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
"""Low-rank matrix completion with PyLops + PyProximal.

Objective
    min_X  1/2 || R vec(X) - y ||_2^2  +  g(sigma(X))
R = pylops.Restriction (sampling of the observed entries).

Stage 1 (convex):     g = lam1 * ||X||_*            (pyproximal.Nuclear)
Stage 2 (non-convex): g = sum_i SCAD_lam2(sigma_i)  (pyproximal.SingularValuePenalty
                      with pyproximal.SCAD), warm-started from stage 1. SCAD
                      thresholds small singular values like the nuclear norm but
                      does not shrink large ones, removing the nuclear-norm bias.

Solver: ProximalGradient with FISTA acceleration (smooth L2 data term + cheap
SVD-based prox). Step tau = 0.99/||R||^2, ||R||^2 from power iteration (= 1).
lam1, lam2: 5-fold cross-validation on the *observed* entries only
(neither Xtrue nor the true rank is used anywhere in the solve).
"""
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
from pylops.optimization.eigs import power_iteration
from pylops.utils.metrics import snr

rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
n = 60
Xtrue = rng.standard_normal((n, 3)) @ rng.standard_normal((3, n))
idx = np.sort(rng.choice(n * n, int(0.3 * n * n), replace=False))  # observed entries (flat indices)
y = Xtrue.ravel()[idx] + 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(idx.size)


def solve(iava, yobs, proxg, x0, niter, tol=1e-10, track=False):
    """FISTA on 1/2||R x - y||^2 + g(x)."""
    R = pylops.Restriction(n * n, iava, dtype="float64")
    L = float(np.real(power_iteration(R.H @ R, niter=50)[0]))
    tau = 0.99 / L
    proxf = pyproximal.L2(Op=R, b=yobs)
    cost = []
    cb = (lambda x: cost.append(proxf(x) + proxg(x))) if track else None
    x = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
        proxf, proxg, x0=x0.copy(), tau=tau, niter=niter, tol=tol,
        acceleration="fista", callback=cb)
    return x, tau, cost


def nuclear(lam):
    return pyproximal.Nuclear((n, n), sigma=float(lam))


def scad(lam):
    return pyproximal.SingularValuePenalty((n, n), pyproximal.SCAD(sigma=float(lam)))


# ---------------- cross-validation on observed entries ----------------
Xzf = np.zeros(n * n)
Xzf[idx] = y
smax = np.linalg.svd(Xzf.reshape(n, n), compute_uv=False)[0]
lams1 = smax * np.logspace(-3, -0.5, 10)   # scale: fraction of top singular value
lams2 = smax * np.logspace(-2.5, -1, 8)

K = 5
folds = np.array_split(np.random.default_rng(1).permutation(idx.size), K)
err1 = np.zeros(len(lams1))
err2 = np.zeros(len(lams2))
for k in range(K):
    te = np.sort(folds[k])
    tr = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), te)
    # stage 1 path, large -> small lam with warm starts
    xw, x1 = np.zeros(n * n), {}
    for il in range(len(lams1) - 1, -1, -1):
        xw, _, _ = solve(idx[tr], y[tr], nuclear(lams1[il]), xw, niter=400)
        x1[il] = xw
        err1[il] += np.sum((xw[idx[te]] - y[te]) ** 2)
    # stage 2 from the fold's best stage-1 solution
    x1best = x1[int(np.argmin([np.sum((x1[i][idx[te]] - y[te]) ** 2)
                               for i in range(len(lams1))]))]
    for il, lam in enumerate(lams2):
        x2, _, _ = solve(idx[tr], y[tr], scad(lam), x1best, niter=400)
        err2[il] += np.sum((x2[idx[te]] - y[te]) ** 2)

cv1 = np.sqrt(err1 / idx.size)
cv2 = np.sqrt(err2 / idx.size)
lam1 = lams1[np.argmin(cv1)]
lam2 = lams2[np.argmin(cv2)]
print("Stage 1 (nuclear)      lam   CV-RMSE")
for l, e in zip(lams1, cv1):
    print(f"                   {l:7.3f}   {e:.4f}{'  <--' if l == lam1 else ''}")
print("Stage 2 (SCAD on sv)   lam   CV-RMSE")
for l, e in zip(lams2, cv2):
    print(f"                   {l:7.3f}   {e:.4f}{'  <--' if l == lam2 else ''}")

# ---------------- final solves on all observed entries ----------------
x1, tau, cost1 = solve(idx, y, nuclear(lam1), np.zeros(n * n), niter=1500, track=True)
xinv, _, cost2 = solve(idx, y, scad(lam2), x1, niter=1500, track=True)
Xinv = xinv.reshape(n, n)


def report(name, x, cost):
    sv = np.linalg.svd(x.reshape(n, n), compute_uv=False)
    res = np.linalg.norm(x[idx] - y) / np.sqrt(idx.size)
    print(f"{name}: iters={len(cost)}, cost {cost[0]:.4f} -> {cost[-1]:.4f} "
          f"(last rel. change {abs(cost[-1] - cost[-2]) / abs(cost[-1]):.1e}), "
          f"rank={(sv > 1e-6 * sv[0]).sum()}, RMS residual={res:.4f}, "
          f"SNR={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), x):.2f} dB")


print(f"\ntau = {tau:.3f}  (0.99/||R||^2)")
print(f"zero-filled baseline: SNR={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xzf):.2f} dB")
report("stage 1 nuclear    ", x1, cost1)
report("stage 2 SCAD (final)", xinv, cost2)

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(14, 4))
    for c, lab in [(cost1, "nuclear"), (cost2, "SCAD")]:
        c = np.asarray(c)
        axs[0].semilogy(c - c.min() + 1e-12, label=lab)
    axs[0].set(title="cost - min(cost)", xlabel="iteration")
    axs[0].legend()
    axs[1].semilogx(lams1, cv1, "o-", label="nuclear")
    axs[1].semilogx(lams2, cv2, "s-", label="SCAD")
    axs[1].set(title="5-fold CV RMSE (observed entries)", xlabel="lam")
    axs[1].legend()
    axs[2].semilogy(np.linalg.svd(Xinv, compute_uv=False), "o")
    axs[2].set(title="singular values of Xinv")
    fig.tight_layout()
    fig.savefig("invert_diagnostics.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

print(f"SNR_DB={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xinv.ravel())}")

Objective
    min_X  1/2 || R vec(X) - y ||_2^2  +  g(sigma(X))
R = pylops.Restriction (sampling of the observed entries).

Stage 1 (convex):     g = lam1 * ||X||_*            (pyproximal.Nuclear)

                      thresholds small singular values like the nuclear norm but
                      does not shrink large ones, removing the nuclear-norm bias.

SVD-based prox). Step tau = 0.99/||R||^2, ||R||^2 from power iteration (= 1).

err2 = np.zeros(len(lams2))

    te = np.sort(folds[k])
    tr = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), te)
    # stage 1 path, large -> small lam with warm starts
    xw, x1 = np.zeros(n * n), {}
    for il in range(len(lams1) - 1, -1, -1):
        xw, _, _ = solve(idx[tr], y[tr], nuclear(lams1[il]), xw, niter=400)
        x1[il] = xw
        err1[il] += np.sum((xw[idx[te]] - y[te]) ** 2)
    # stage 2 from the fold's best stage-1 solution
    x1best = x1[int(np.argmin([np.sum((x1[i][idx[te]] - y[te]) ** 2)
                               for i in range(len(lams1))]))]
    for il, lam in enumerate(lams2):
        x2, _, _ = solve(idx[tr], y[tr], scad(lam), x1best, niter=400)
        err2[il] += np.sum((x2[idx[te]] - y[te]) ** 2)

cv1 = np.sqrt(err1 / idx.size)
cv2 = np.sqrt(err2 / idx.size)
lam1 = lams1[np.argmin(cv1)]
lam2 = lams2[np.argmin(cv2)]
print("Stage 1 (nuclear)      lam   CV-RMSE")
for l, e in zip(lams1, cv1):
    print(f"                   {l:7.3f}   {e:.4f}{'  <--' if l == lam1 else ''}")
print("Stage 2 (SCAD on sv)   lam   CV-RMSE")
for l, e in zip(lams2, cv2):
    print(f"                   {l:7.3f}   {e:.4f}{'  <--' if l == lam2 else ''}")

# ---------------- final solves on all observed entries ----------------
x1, tau, cost1 = solve(idx, y, nuclear(lam1), np.zeros(n * n), niter=1500, track=True)
xinv, _, cost2 = solve(idx, y, scad(lam2), x1, niter=1500, track=True)
Xinv = xinv.reshape(n, n)


def report(name, x, cost):
    sv = np.linalg.svd(x.reshape(n, n), compute_uv=False)
    res = np.linalg.norm(x[idx] - y) / np.sqrt(idx.size)
    print(f"{name}: iters={len(cost)}, cost {cost[0]:.4f} -> {cost[-1]:.4f} "
          f"(last rel. change {abs(cost[-1] - cost[-2]) / abs(cost[-1]):.1e}), "
          f"rank={(sv > 1e-6 * sv[0]).sum()}, RMS residual={res:.4f}, "
          f"SNR={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), x):.2f} dB")


print(f"\ntau = {tau:.3f}  (0.99/||R||^2)")
print(f"zero-filled baseline: SNR={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xzf):.2f} dB")
report("stage 1 nuclear    ", x1, cost1)
report("stage 2 SCAD (final)", xinv, cost2)

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(14, 4))
    for c, lab in [(cost1, "nuclear"), (cost2, "SCAD")]:
        c = np.asarray(c)
        axs[0].semilogy(c - c.min() + 1e-12, label=lab)
    axs[0].set(title="cost - min(cost)", xlabel="iteration")
    axs[0].legend()
    axs[1].semilogx(lams1, cv1, "o-", label="nuclear")
    axs[1].semilogx(lams2, cv2, "s-", label="SCAD")
    axs[1].set(title="5-fold CV RMSE (observed entries)", xlabel="lam")
    axs[1].legend()
    axs[2].semilogy(np.linalg.svd(Xinv, compute_uv=False), "o")
    axs[2].set(title="singular values of Xinv")
    fig.tight_layout()
    fig.savefig("invert_diagnostics.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

print(f"SNR_DB={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xinv.ravel())}")

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I saved and ran `invert.py`, and it fills in the table well: **`SNR_DB=22.3224`** on the full 60 × 60 matrix. For comparison, filling the missing entries with zeros gives 1.53 dB. The script takes about 25 s and also saves a cost curve and side-by-side images to `invert.png`.

## What I chose

**Objective:** min_X ½‖R vec(X) − y‖² + λ‖X‖_*
- `R = pylops.Restriction(3600, idx)` picks out the observed entries, so the data term is `pyproximal.L2(Op=R, b=y)`. It is smooth.
- The nuclear norm `pyproximal.Nuclear((60, 60), sigma=λ)` (the sum of singular values) pushes the result toward a matrix explained by a few factors, which is your assumption.

**Solver:** `ProximalGradient(..., acceleration="fista")` from PyProximal. It is the standard fit when one term is smooth and the other has a cheap proximal step (here, shrinking the singular values).
- **Step size:** τ = 0.99/‖RᴴR‖. Power iteration measured ‖RᴴR‖ = 1, so τ = 0.99.
- **Convergence:** over 500 iterations the cost fell from 27.3 to 15.61. Its relative change over the last 50 iterations was 2.3e-6.

**Choosing λ without using the true matrix:** I used 5-fold cross-validation on the observed entries only. I tried 12 values of λ on a log scale below λ_max = 28.0 (λ_max is the value at which the solution becomes all zeros). The held-out error is flat for small λ and lowest at **λ = 0.087**, with held-out RMSE 0.374.

**Refit step (PyLops):** shrinking singular values also pulls the large ones toward zero and leaves a tail of small ones. The nuclear-norm solution's singular values are 78.7, 49.3, 40.9, then 2.34, 0.96, … and 21 are nonzero in total.
- I set the rank r at the largest gap between consecutive nonzero singular values, which gives **r = 3**. The number comes from the solution, not from knowing how the synthetic was built.
- I then kept the top 3 singular vectors U, V and refit the 3 × 3 core S by least squares on the observed entries (`Restriction @ Kronecker(U, V)`, solved with `lsqr`).
- The script keeps the refit only if cross-validation favours it. It did: held-out RMSE dropped from 0.376 to 0.302.
- My first version of this rule picked r = 21, because it found the jump between the real singular values and the exact zeros. Cross-validation showed almost no gain for that refit; I fixed the rule and reran.

## Results

| Method | SNR (dB) | RMS misfit on observed entries |
|---|---|---|
| Zero-fill | 1.53 | – |
| Nuclear norm (λ from CV) | 20.72 | 0.015 (fitting the noise) |
| **Nuclear norm + rank-3 refit (final `Xinv`)** | **22.32** | 0.071 (close to the noise level) |

## Things to know for your real data
- **λ is not finely determined.** The cross-validation curve is flat over roughly 0.03–0.15, so the λ grid barely matters. The refit step is what removes the remaining bias.
- **The refit depends on a visible gap in the singular values.** Here it was sharp (40.9 → 2.3). If your real table has no clear gap, the script falls back on the cross-validation check, which will keep the plain nuclear-norm solution if the refit doesn't predict held-out entries better.
- **Possible upgrade:** a non-convex rank penalty such as `pyproximal.QuadraticEnvelopeRankL2` or `SingularValuePenalty` could do the same job in one step. I didn't try it.

**Objective:** min_X ½‖R vec(X) − y‖² + λ‖X‖_*

"""Low-rank matrix completion with PyLops + PyProximal.

Objective:   min_X  1/2 || R vec(X) - y ||_2^2  +  lam * ||X||_*
  R    : pylops.Restriction selecting the observed entries (||R|| = 1)
  ||.||_* : nuclear norm (prox = singular value soft-thresholding)
Solver: ProximalGradient with FISTA acceleration, tau = 1/||R||^2 = 1.
lam   : chosen by 5-fold cross-validation on the *observed* entries only.
"""
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
from pylops.optimization.eigs import power_iteration

rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
n = 60
Xtrue = rng.standard_normal((n, 3)) @ rng.standard_normal((3, n))
idx = np.sort(rng.choice(n * n, int(0.3 * n * n), replace=False))  # observed entries (flat indices)
y = Xtrue.ravel()[idx] + 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(idx.size)

NITER = 500


def complete(iava, yobs, lam, niter=NITER, x0=None, callback=None):
    """Solve 1/2||R x - y||^2 + lam ||X||_* with FISTA."""
    R = pylops.Restriction(n * n, iava, dtype="float64")
    proxf = pyproximal.L2(Op=R, b=yobs)
    proxg = pyproximal.Nuclear((n, n), sigma=float(lam))
    if x0 is None:
        x0 = np.zeros(n * n)
    # step size from the operator norm: L = ||R^H R|| (= 1 for a restriction)
    L = power_iteration(R.H @ R, niter=50)[0].real
    tau = 0.99 / L
    x = pyproximal.optimization.primal.ProximalGradient(
        proxf, proxg, x0=x0, tau=tau, niter=niter, acceleration="fista",
        callback=callback)
    return x, R, proxf, proxg, tau


def gap_rank(X):
    """Rank at the largest ratio gap s_k / s_{k+1} among the nonzero singular
    values (the exact zeros produced by soft-thresholding are excluded)."""
    s = np.linalg.svd(X, compute_uv=False)
    s = s[s > 1e-6 * s[0]]
    if s.size < 2:
        return s.size
    return int(np.argmax(s[:-1] / s[1:])) + 1


def debias(X, iava, yobs):
    """Keep the rank-r subspaces of X (r from the singular-value gap) and refit
    the r x r core S by least squares on the observed data:
        min_S || R (U kron V) vec(S) - y ||^2,   X = U S V^T."""
    r = gap_rank(X)
    U, _, Vt = np.linalg.svd(X)
    Ur, Vr = U[:, :r], Vt[:r].T
    Op = pylops.Restriction(n * n, iava, dtype="float64") @ pylops.Kronecker(
        pylops.MatrixMult(Ur), pylops.MatrixMult(Vr))
    S = pylops.optimization.basic.lsqr(Op, yobs, niter=100, atol=1e-12, btol=1e-12)[0]
    return (Ur @ S.reshape(r, r) @ Vr.T).ravel(), r


# --- choose lam by K-fold cross-validation on observed data (no truth used) ---
Rfull = pylops.Restriction(n * n, idx, dtype="float64")
lam_max = np.linalg.norm((Rfull.H @ y).reshape(n, n), 2)  # lam >= lam_max -> X = 0
lams = lam_max * np.logspace(-3, -0.3, 12)
K = 5
perm = np.random.default_rng(123).permutation(idx.size)
folds = np.array_split(perm, K)

cv_err = np.zeros(len(lams))
for k, test in enumerate(folds):
    train = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), test)
    xw = None
    for il, lam in enumerate(lams[::-1]):  # large -> small lam, warm start
        xw, *_ = complete(idx[train], y[train], lam, niter=300, x0=xw)
        cv_err[len(lams) - 1 - il] += np.mean((xw[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / K
ibest = int(np.argmin(cv_err))
lam = lams[ibest]
print("lam_max = %.3f" % lam_max)
print(" lam       CV-RMSE (held-out observed entries)")
for l, e in zip(lams, cv_err):
    print(" %8.4f  %.4f%s" % (l, np.sqrt(e), "  <- chosen" if l == lam else ""))

# --- CV check: does the rank-r debiasing refit predict held-out data better? ---
cv_nuc, cv_deb = 0.0, 0.0
for test in folds:
    train = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), test)
    xn, *_ = complete(idx[train], y[train], lam, niter=NITER)
    xd, _ = debias(xn.reshape(n, n), idx[train], y[train])
    cv_nuc += np.mean((xn[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / K
    cv_deb += np.mean((xd[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / K
use_debias = cv_deb < cv_nuc
print("CV-RMSE nuclear only: %.4f   nuclear + rank-r refit: %.4f  -> %s"
      % (np.sqrt(cv_nuc), np.sqrt(cv_deb), "use refit" if use_debias else "keep nuclear"))

# --- final solve on all observed entries ---
cost = []
R = Rfull


def cb(x):
    cost.append(0.5 * np.sum((x[idx] - y) ** 2) +
                lam * np.sum(np.linalg.svd(x.reshape(n, n), compute_uv=False)))


x, R, proxf, proxg, tau = complete(idx, y, lam, callback=cb)
Xinv = x.reshape(n, n)
cost = np.array(cost)
print("tau = %.3f  (||R||^2 = 1 from power iteration)" % tau)
print("iterations: %d   cost start %.4e  end %.4e  rel. change last 50 its %.2e"
      % (len(cost), cost[0], cost[-1], abs(cost[-51] - cost[-1]) / cost[-1]))

s = np.linalg.svd(Xinv, compute_uv=False)
print("leading singular values (nuclear solution):", np.round(s[:6], 3))
print("numerical rank of nuclear solution (s > 1e-6 s_max): %d" % np.sum(s > 1e-6 * s[0]))
print("residual RMS on observed entries: %.4f" % np.sqrt(np.mean((Xinv.ravel()[idx] - y) ** 2)))

Xnuc = Xinv.copy()
print("nuclear-norm solution SNR: %.2f dB"
      % pylops.utils.metrics.snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xnuc.ravel()))
if use_debias:
    xd, r = debias(Xnuc, idx, y)
    Xinv = xd.reshape(n, n)
    print("rank-%d refit: residual RMS on observed entries %.4f"
          % (r, np.sqrt(np.mean((Xinv.ravel()[idx] - y) ** 2))))

# baseline: zero-filled matrix (no prior)
Xzf = (R.H @ y).reshape(n, n)
print("baseline zero-fill SNR: %.2f dB" % pylops.utils.metrics.snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xzf.ravel()))

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 4, figsize=(16, 3.5))
    ax[0].semilogy(cost); ax[0].set_title("cost"); ax[0].set_xlabel("iteration")
    for a, im, t in zip(ax[1:], [Xtrue, Xzf, Xinv], ["true", "observed (zero-fill)", "completed"]):
        a.imshow(im, cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=-np.abs(Xtrue).max(), vmax=np.abs(Xtrue).max())
        a.set_title(t)
    fig.tight_layout(); fig.savefig("invert.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

print("SNR_DB=%.4f" % pylops.utils.metrics.snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xinv.ravel()))

Objective:   min_X  1/2 || R vec(X) - y ||_2^2  +  lam * ||X||_*
  R    : pylops.Restriction selecting the observed entries (||R|| = 1)
  ||.||_* : nuclear norm (prox = singular value soft-thresholding)

    values (the exact zeros produced by soft-thresholding are excluded)."""

    if s.size < 2:
        return s.size
    return int(np.argmax(s[:-1] / s[1:])) + 1

    """Keep the rank-r subspaces of X (r from the singular-value gap) and refit
    the r x r core S by least squares on the observed data:
        min_S || R (U kron V) vec(S) - y ||^2,   X = U S V^T."""

    U, _, Vt = np.linalg.svd(X)
    Ur, Vr = U[:, :r], Vt[:r].T

        pylops.MatrixMult(Ur), pylops.MatrixMult(Vr))
    S = pylops.optimization.basic.lsqr(Op, yobs, niter=100, atol=1e-12, btol=1e-12)[0]
    return (Ur @ S.reshape(r, r) @ Vr.T).ravel(), r


# --- choose lam by K-fold cross-validation on observed data (no truth used) ---
Rfull = pylops.Restriction(n * n, idx, dtype="float64")
lam_max = np.linalg.norm((Rfull.H @ y).reshape(n, n), 2)  # lam >= lam_max -> X = 0
lams = lam_max * np.logspace(-3, -0.3, 12)
K = 5
perm = np.random.default_rng(123).permutation(idx.size)
folds = np.array_split(perm, K)

cv_err = np.zeros(len(lams))
for k, test in enumerate(folds):
    train = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), test)
    xw = None
    for il, lam in enumerate(lams[::-1]):  # large -> small lam, warm start
        xw, *_ = complete(idx[train], y[train], lam, niter=300, x0=xw)
        cv_err[len(lams) - 1 - il] += np.mean((xw[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / K
ibest = int(np.argmin(cv_err))
lam = lams[ibest]
print("lam_max = %.3f" % lam_max)
print(" lam       CV-RMSE (held-out observed entries)")
for l, e in zip(lams, cv_err):
    print(" %8.4f  %.4f%s" % (l, np.sqrt(e), "  <- chosen" if l == lam else ""))

# --- CV check: does the rank-r debiasing refit predict held-out data better? ---
cv_nuc, cv_deb = 0.0, 0.0
for test in folds:
    train = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), test)
    xn, *_ = complete(idx[train], y[train], lam, niter=NITER)
    xd, _ = debias(xn.reshape(n, n), idx[train], y[train])
    cv_nuc += np.mean((xn[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / K
    cv_deb += np.mean((xd[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / K
use_debias = cv_deb < cv_nuc
print("CV-RMSE nuclear only: %.4f   nuclear + rank-r refit: %.4f  -> %s"
      % (np.sqrt(cv_nuc), np.sqrt(cv_deb), "use refit" if use_debias else "keep nuclear"))

# --- final solve on all observed entries ---
cost = []
R = Rfull


def cb(x):
    cost.append(0.5 * np.sum((x[idx] - y) ** 2) +
                lam * np.sum(np.linalg.svd(x.reshape(n, n), compute_uv=False)))


x, R, proxf, proxg, tau = complete(idx, y, lam, callback=cb)
Xinv = x.reshape(n, n)
cost = np.array(cost)
print("tau = %.3f  (||R||^2 = 1 from power iteration)" % tau)
print("iterations: %d   cost start %.4e  end %.4e  rel. change last 50 its %.2e"
      % (len(cost), cost[0], cost[-1], abs(cost[-51] - cost[-1]) / cost[-1]))

s = np.linalg.svd(Xinv, compute_uv=False)
print("leading singular values (nuclear solution):", np.round(s[:6], 3))
print("numerical rank of nuclear solution (s > 1e-6 s_max): %d" % np.sum(s > 1e-6 * s[0]))
print("residual RMS on observed entries: %.4f" % np.sqrt(np.mean((Xinv.ravel()[idx] - y) ** 2)))

Xnuc = Xinv.copy()
print("nuclear-norm solution SNR: %.2f dB"
      % pylops.utils.metrics.snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xnuc.ravel()))
if use_debias:
    xd, r = debias(Xnuc, idx, y)
    Xinv = xd.reshape(n, n)
    print("rank-%d refit: residual RMS on observed entries %.4f"
          % (r, np.sqrt(np.mean((Xinv.ravel()[idx] - y) ** 2))))

# baseline: zero-filled matrix (no prior)
Xzf = (R.H @ y).reshape(n, n)
print("baseline zero-fill SNR: %.2f dB" % pylops.utils.metrics.snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xzf.ravel()))

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 4, figsize=(16, 3.5))
    ax[0].semilogy(cost); ax[0].set_title("cost"); ax[0].set_xlabel("iteration")
    for a, im, t in zip(ax[1:], [Xtrue, Xzf, Xinv], ["true", "observed (zero-fill)", "completed"]):
        a.imshow(im, cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=-np.abs(Xtrue).max(), vmax=np.abs(Xtrue).max())
        a.set_title(t)
    fig.tight_layout(); fig.savefig("invert.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

print("SNR_DB=%.4f" % pylops.utils.metrics.snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xinv.ravel()))
✓ pass trigger plugin-fired indicator

Skill called 1x (expected 1..∞)

Run 3 100% 16 turns · $0.63 · 15:31:33 UTC
✓ pass convergence-reported ×0.25

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I wrote `invert.py`, ran it (about 15 s), and it ends with **`SNR_DB=33.83`**.

### What I chose
**Approach:** treat it as least squares on the recorded entries plus a penalty that favours a matrix built from few factors. `x` is the flattened matrix and `R = pylops.Restriction` picks out the 1080 recorded entries. The data term is `f = pyproximal.L2(Op=R, b=y)`, solved with PyProximal's `ProximalGradient` in two stages:

1. **Nuclear norm** (convex): `min ½‖Rx − y‖² + λ‖X‖_*`, using `pyproximal.Nuclear` and the accelerated (FISTA) solver. This is the standard way to fill in a low-rank matrix.
2. **Rank penalty** (non-convex): `min ½‖Rx − y‖² + σ·rank(X)`, using `pyproximal.SingularValuePenalty(L0)`. It starts from the stage-1 result and uses the plain, non-accelerated solver.

**Why the second stage:** stage 1 alone reached only 20.4 dB. The nuclear norm shrinks every factor it keeps, so its predictions on held-out entries stayed about 40× worse than the noise level. I checked it wasn't a convergence problem: running 10× longer changed the cost by only ~1e-9. The rank penalty either keeps a factor whole or drops it, so the kept factors aren't shrunk. Starting from stage 1 avoids the bad local minima a non-convex penalty can fall into.

**Step size:** `τ = 0.99/‖R‖²`. I computed `‖R‖² = 1.000` by power iteration, which is as expected for an operator that just selects entries.

**Choosing λ and σ without the true matrix:** 5-fold cross-validation on the recorded entries. Each candidate is fitted on 4/5 of them and scored on predicting the other 1/5. The rank of 3 is never given to the script; it comes out of the cross-validation.
- λ = 0.028. The stage-1 cross-validation curve is flat and fairly poor, and 0.028 is the smallest value I tried, so λ isn't really pinned down. That doesn't matter much because stage 1 is only the starting point.
- σ = 5. Cross-validation error is 0.0047 for σ from 5 to 20, all giving rank 3, against 0.099 or worse elsewhere. Where several values tie, the script takes the smallest. The noise variance is 0.0025, so 0.0047 is close to the best you can expect.

### How well it worked
| Method | SNR vs. true matrix |
|---|---|
| Missing entries set to zero | 1.53 dB |
| Nuclear norm only (stage 1) | 20.38 dB |
| **Nuclear norm → rank penalty (final)** | **33.83 dB** |

- **Convergence:** stage 1 ran 1000 iterations, with the cost going from 8.998 to 5.068. Stage 2 stopped on its tolerance after 603 iterations, with the cost going from 35.87 to 32.89 and decreasing throughout. The costs are also plotted in `invert_diagnostics.png`.
- **Result:** the final matrix has exactly rank 3, with singular values 80.4, 54.1 and 42.8 and the rest 0.
- **Fit to the data:** the error on the recorded entries is 0.041. That is about what you'd expect with noise of 0.05 and ~350 fitted degrees of freedom (≈ 0.05·√(1 − 351/1080) ≈ 0.041), so it is neither overfitting nor underfitting.

### Notes for your real data
- The rank penalty is non-convex, so the result depends on the stage-1 starting point. Here the right rank showed up clearly over a wide range of σ. Check that the cross-validation plateau is just as clear on your real table.
- The candidate grids for λ and σ are hard-coded. If your values are on a different scale, the σ grid in particular may need adjusting.
- The cross-validation tuning takes most of the ~15 s run time.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
"""Low-rank matrix completion with PyLops + PyProximal.

x = vec(X), X is 60x60, R = pylops.Restriction selecting the observed entries.

Stage 1 (convex, initialiser):
    min_x  1/2 ||R x - y||^2 + lam * ||X||_*          (nuclear norm)
Stage 2 (non-convex, debiasing), warm-started from stage 1:
    min_x  1/2 ||R x - y||^2 + sig * rank(X)          (L0 on singular values)

Both: f = L2(Op=R, b=y) smooth with Lipschitz constant ||R||^2 = 1 -> ProximalGradient,
tau = 0.99 / ||R||^2. g = Nuclear (soft-thresholds singular values) or
SingularValuePenalty(L0) (hard-thresholds them, so kept factors are not shrunk).
FISTA for the convex stage, plain proximal gradient for the non-convex stage.

lam and sig are chosen without the true matrix: 5-fold cross-validation on the
observed entries (fit on 4/5, predict the held-out 1/5), then refit on all data.
"""
import numpy as np
import pylops
import pyproximal
from pylops.optimization.eigs import power_iteration
from pyproximal.optimization.primal import ProximalGradient

rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
n = 60
Xtrue = rng.standard_normal((n, 3)) @ rng.standard_normal((3, n))
idx = np.sort(rng.choice(n * n, int(0.3 * n * n), replace=False))  # observed entries (flat indices)
y = Xtrue.ravel()[idx] + 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(idx.size)


def solve(iava, yobs, lam, sig, x0=None, niter1=500, niter2=500, track=False):
    """Stage 1 nuclear-norm FISTA, then stage 2 rank-penalised proximal gradient."""
    R = pylops.Restriction(n * n, iava, dtype="float64")
    L = np.real(power_iteration(R.H @ R, niter=50)[0])  # = 1 for a restriction
    tau = 0.99 / L
    f = pyproximal.L2(Op=R, b=yobs)
    g1 = pyproximal.Nuclear((n, n), sigma=float(lam))
    c1, c2 = [], []
    x1 = ProximalGradient(f, g1, np.zeros(n * n) if x0 is None else x0, tau=tau,
                          niter=niter1, acceleration="fista", tol=1e-10,
                          callback=(lambda x: c1.append(f(x) + g1(x))) if track else None)
    if sig is None:
        return x1, None, (c1, c2, tau, L)
    g2 = pyproximal.SingularValuePenalty((n, n), pyproximal.L0(sigma=float(sig)))
    x2 = ProximalGradient(f, g2, x1.copy(), tau=tau, niter=niter2, tol=1e-12,
                          callback=(lambda x: c2.append(f(x) + g2(x))) if track else None)
    return x1, x2, (c1, c2, tau, L)


folds = np.array_split(rng.permutation(idx.size), 5)


def cv_error(fn):
    err = 0.0
    for test in folds:
        train = np.setdiff1d(np.arange(idx.size), test)
        x = fn(idx[train], y[train])
        err += np.mean((x[idx[test]] - y[test]) ** 2) / len(folds)
    return err


# ---- stage 1: choose lam by CV ----
lam_max = np.linalg.norm(np.bincount(idx, weights=y, minlength=n * n).reshape(n, n), 2)
lams = lam_max * np.logspace(-3, -0.3, 10)
cv1 = [cv_error(lambda i, d: solve(i, d, lam, None, niter1=300)[0]) for lam in lams]
lam = lams[int(np.argmin(cv1))]
print("Stage 1 nuclear norm:  lambda -> CV-MSE")
for l_, c_ in zip(lams, cv1):
    print(f"  {l_:9.4f}  {c_:.5f}{'  <- chosen' if l_ == lam else ''}")

# ---- stage 2: choose sig (rank-penalty weight) by CV ----
sigs = np.array([0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 50.0])
cv2 = [cv_error(lambda i, d: solve(i, d, lam, s, niter1=300, niter2=300)[1]) for s in sigs]
# smallest CV error; among values within 1% of it take the smallest penalty
ok = np.array(cv2) <= 1.01 * min(cv2)
sig = sigs[ok][0]
print("Stage 2 rank penalty:  sigma -> CV-MSE")
for s_, c_ in zip(sigs, cv2):
    print(f"  {s_:9.4f}  {c_:.5f}{'  <- chosen' if s_ == sig else ''}")

# ---- final solve on all observed entries ----
x1, xinv, (c1, c2, tau, L) = solve(idx, y, lam, sig, niter1=1000, niter2=1000, track=True)
Xinv = xinv.reshape(n, n)
print(f"\n||R||^2 = {L:.4f}, tau = {tau:.4f}, lam = {lam:.4f}, sig = {sig}")
print(f"stage 1: {len(c1)} its, cost {c1[0]:.4e} -> {c1[-1]:.4e}")
print(f"stage 2: {len(c2)} its, cost {c2[0]:.4e} -> {c2[-1]:.4e}")
sv = np.linalg.svd(Xinv, compute_uv=False)
print("leading singular values of Xinv:", np.round(sv[:5], 3),
      "| rank:", int((sv > 1e-6 * sv[0]).sum()))
print(f"data residual RMS on observed entries = {np.sqrt(np.mean((xinv[idx] - y) ** 2)):.4f}")

X0 = np.zeros(n * n)
X0[idx] = y
snr = pylops.utils.metrics.snr
print(f"SNR zero-fill baseline   = {snr(Xtrue.ravel(), X0):.2f} dB")
print(f"SNR nuclear norm only    = {snr(Xtrue.ravel(), x1):.2f} dB")

try:
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use("Agg")
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(14, 4))
    ax[0].semilogy(c1, label="stage 1 (nuclear)"); ax[0].semilogy(c2, label="stage 2 (rank)")
    ax[0].set_xlabel("iteration"); ax[0].set_ylabel("objective"); ax[0].legend()
    ax[1].semilogx(lams, cv1, "o-"); ax[1].axvline(lam, color="k", ls="--")
    ax[1].set_xlabel("lambda"); ax[1].set_ylabel("CV MSE")
    ax[2].semilogx(sigs, cv2, "o-"); ax[2].axvline(sig, color="k", ls="--")
    ax[2].set_xlabel("sigma (rank penalty)"); ax[2].set_ylabel("CV MSE")
    fig.tight_layout(); fig.savefig("invert_diagnostics.png", dpi=100)
except ImportError:
    pass

print(f"SNR_DB={snr(Xtrue.ravel(), Xinv.ravel())}")
